# Ramesh Babu: From Barber to Rolls-Royce
Runtime 45:00 · American English · Standard mode, delivered in one pass · Stages S0 and S1

---

## S0: Intake, media math and risk scan

### S0-A. Brief

| Setting | Choice | Basis |

| Topic | Ramesh Babu: From Barber to Rolls-Royce | Production rule 1 |

| Audience | United States, general audience 18+, no prior knowledge of India assumed | Version lock |

| Mode | Standard, delivered in one pass with no chat turns | Production rule 1 |
| Master | 3840×2160 UHD container with a **2.39:1 letterbox** (active picture 3840×1608, bars of 276 px top and bottom), **23.976 fps** | Production rule 5 (replaces the engine's 16:9/25 fps) |
| Cut-downs | 9:16 and 4:5, full frame with no letterbox | (default) |

### S0-A2. Finish lock (applies to every shot; S4 holds the full look bible)

| Item | Recipe |
|---|---|
| Color pipeline | DaVinci Resolve, color-managed (DaVinci YRGB Color Managed). Timeline in DaVinci Wide Gamut / DaVinci Intermediate, output Rec.709 Gamma 2.4. Each clip's input is set to its real color space: most free stock is Rec.709, and log clips are set from their camera tag. |
| Node order | 1 balance (exposure, white balance to neutral) → 2 light temporal noise reduction (cleans up stock compression so the grain sits on a clean image) → 3 match the shot to the chapter's hero frame → 4 shared LOOK group → 5 halation and grain at timeline level |
| Master look LOOK-M ("print film") | Soft S-curve pivoting at DaVinci Intermediate mid-gray (0.336), contrast +10–15%. Shadows pushed slightly toward teal (hue about 195°, low saturation) and highlights toward warm amber (hue about 40°). Global saturation 85–90%. Skin tones protected with an HSL qualifier. Soft highlight roll-off. |
| Print emulation (the LUT) | Resolve's bundled film look "Rec709 Kodak 2383 D65" at 50–60% node key output. A Color Space Transform node (DWG/Intermediate → Rec.709 Gamma 2.4) feeds it, and the output transform is set so the signal is not converted twice. The colorist checks this on a test chart before grading. |
| Chapter trims (on top of LOOK-M, never replacing it) | LOOK-A: sodium-street tungsten warmth for the origin years (CH1–CH2). LOOK-B: neutral daylight for the build years (CH3–CH4). LOOK-C: restrained gold for the present (CH5–CH6). |
| Halation | Gentle, on highlights only: Resolve Studio's Halation effect at low strength. If that is unavailable, a glow on luma above about 85%, tinted red-orange, blended at 10–15%. |
| Grain | Resolve Film Grain, 35mm preset, low strength, **one setting for the whole film**. Past-era scenes get no heavier grain, so no modern clip passes itself off as period archive. |
| Frame-rate conform | 23.976/24 fps sources play native. 25 fps sources are conformed (4% slower; stock audio is muted anyway). 29.97/30 fps sources are conformed to 80% speed or retimed with optical flow. **50/60 fps sources are kept for speed ramps** and 40% slow motion. |
| Push-ins and reframes | Up to 1.15× on 4K sources. Deeper push-ins only on 5K and larger sources (for example Pexels 31741890 at 5120×2700, Pexels 9997960 at 8K). The 2.39 window can also slide up to 552 px (2160 − 1608) vertically for tilt-style reframes. 1080p sources are used only for wide shots, scaled conventionally; no AI upscaling. |
| Edit grammar | Push-ins, reframes, speed ramps, match cuts (scissor blade → car door closing; comb teeth → lane lines; a ledger rule → the road), J and L cuts at every reset |
| Audio finish | About −14 LUFS integrated and −1 dBTP true peak for YouTube. A continuous ambience bed so no gap drops to digital silence. Deliberate 1–2 beat silences before reveals. Music rises and falls on story beats, never on a timer. |

### S0-B. Media math

| Item | Formula | Result |
|---|---|---|
| shots_required | ceil(45 × 4.5 × 1.2) = ceil(243.0) | **243** unique source assets |
| narration_scenes | ceil(45 × 6) | **270** scenes, average 10 s (range 8–12 s) |
| Wordless time | 2,700 s × 8% = 216 s ÷ 10 s | about 22 wordless scenes (silence, music holds, title cards), leaving about 248 narrated |
| word_budget (ceiling) | 45 × 150 | **6,750** words |
| Planned spoken words | 6,750 × 0.92 | about **6,210** words (≈ 248 × 25) |
| Words per scene | 10 s × 2.5 words/s | 25 (20–30 at 8–12 s) |
| Visual events: hook | 0.5 min × 22.5 (midpoint of 15–30) | 11.25 (one cut about every 2.7 s) |
| Visual events: explanation | 15.5 min × 12.5 (10–15) | 193.75 |
| Visual events: evidence | 19 min × 10 (8–12) | 190 |
| Visual events: emotional hold | 10 min × 8 (6–10) | 80 |
| visual_events_total | 11.25 + 193.75 + 190 + 80 (section minutes 0.5 + 15.5 + 19 + 10 = 45) | **475** |
| R1 share (raised, see scarcity check) | 475 × 0.40 | 190 R1 events |
| R3 stock events | 475 − 190 | 285 |
| Unique assets by rung | 148 R3 clips + 95 R1 builds | 243 (matches shots_required) |
| l5_variant_budget | 475 − 243 | **232** = 137 stock variants (285 − 148) + 95 R1 restates (190 − 95) |
| L5 cap check | Stock: 148 × 3 = 444 ≥ 285. R1: 95 × 3 = 285 ≥ 190 | PASS. No clip is used more than 3 times, every variant differs by at least 50% of frame area, and uses of one clip sit at least 10:00 apart |
| segment_resets | floor(45 / 3) | **15** |
| pattern_interrupts | 45 × 2.4 (= 2,700 s ÷ 25 s) | **108** |
| render_chunks | Film is 60 min or shorter, so 1–4 at the pipeline's choice | Pipeline's choice |
| ai_reconstruction_cap | Production rule 2 | **0 s** (the engine's 20% default does not apply) |
| shorts_cutdowns | 2–4 plus 1 Facebook cut | 3 + 1 |
| Long-film blocks | Apply only above 60:00 | Not applicable |

An R1 "restate" is the same build shown with new data, or a reframed region that differs by at least 50% of the frame. An example is the ledger motif gaining its next entry.

**Scarcity check (free stock, R1 + R3 only)**

| Category | Literal sources available | Unique R3 clips needed | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| Photos or footage of Ramesh Babu or his family | **0** | 0 by design | Fewer than 30, so the R1 share rises to 40% of all events. He appears only as hands, the back of a head, a silhouette, POV and objects (scissors, straight razor, steering wheel, keys, ledger). No stock face is ever presented as him. |
| Bengaluru places | 8 leads: Pexels 31741890, 28322564, 30263565, 28837593, 18831305, 30590791; Pixabay 42557; the Videvo Majestic page | 38 | Search further. A clip is captioned "Bengaluru" only when its stock page names the city. Other Indian street clips are unnamed city texture. Named places are carried by OpenStreetMap maps. |
| Barber craft | 3 Pexels leads (34445702, 7697526, 4178140), the Mixkit barber category (about 117 clips), Pixabay search (854+ matches) | 34 | Hands, blades, chair, mirror, towel. Faces cropped out. |
| Vans, driving, keys, traffic | No Maruti Omni clip found | 26 | Details of unbranded vans, driver POV, keys. The Omni's shape becomes an R1 line drawing labeled "Illustration". |
| Luxury cars | 7 Pexels leads, Pixabay (91+), the Videvo page | 24 | Details only (paint, wheel, door, interior). Labeled "Illustrative" wherever the narration names his car. **Kept out of every slot about his Ghost:** Pixabay 44911 (a 3D render), Pexels 11095144 (a convertible, a different model), Pexels 18837288 (a classic model). The Videvo "Rodeo Drive" clip is probably editorial: check before use. |
| Money, ledgers, objects | Plentiful generic stock | 14 | Hands counting, ledger, pen, receipts |
| Tech-hub context (the Intel era) | 1 lead (Pexels 30590791) | 12 | No Intel footage or logo. Intel is named in typography only. |
| Context from 1979 to the 1990s | 0 (archive is outside the ladder) | 0 | R1 timeline and period typography. Present-day textures are shot close so they read timeless. Modern footage is never passed off as the 1990s. |
| **R1 builds** | Owned | 95 | OSM maps 10 · ledger/timeline states 25 · data charts 20 · document-style cards 10 · attributed paraphrase cards 20 · conversion/method cards 10 |

Stock clips needed: 38 + 34 + 26 + 24 + 14 + 12 = **148**.

**How to find less common stock (production rule 5)**
1. Search with 2–4 plain nouns plus a light word, for example "straight razor leather strop close", "old barber chair chrome footrest", "steering wheel hands night rain", "car keys wooden counter", "ledger handwriting fountain pen top view". Place names are used only for place-literal searches. Go past the first page of results. On Pixabay, sort by newest rather than popular. On Pexels, filter to 4K and browse contributor profiles (for example Anil Sharma's Bengaluru clips). On Videvo, filter to the Free license. Browse Mixkit and Coverr category pages by hand, past the first screen.
2. Run one frame from each candidate through a reverse image search (Google Lens or TinEye). Drop the clip if that frame appears across many YouTube thumbnails or ads.
3. Prefer clips with practical light, slight handheld movement, shallow depth of field, people who are incidental or unidentifiable, and 4K or higher resolution. Reject clips with looks to camera, posed smiles, the over-saturated "commercial" grade, burned-in text or watermarks.
4. Mute every stock clip and rebuild the ambience from Pixabay and Mixkit SFX.
5. Log each clip: URL, contributor, license page saved as PDF, download date, resolution, fps.

### S0-C. Template and arc

**Template:** 45:00 falls in the 45:00–59:59 slot, a chaptered saga of self-contained chapters. One film-level loop ("Why is he still cutting hair?") runs through every chapter.

**Arc:** Success story (origin → the bet → near-failure → breakthrough → cost → transferable lesson). It opens cold on a defining moment, the way the biography arc does. Production rule 6 adds two turns, both real events in the record:
- a **midpoint turn at 22:30**: in 2004, as ordinary car rental filled with competitors, he moves into luxury cars;
- an **80% cliffhanger at 36:00**: the Rolls-Royce tax bill that, by his own account, left him almost bankrupt.

**Chapter skeleton** (provisional; S3 binds it):

| Chapter | Title | Time |
|---|---|---|
| CH0 | Cold open and proof | 0:00–1:15 |
| CH1 | The Shop | 1:15–8:30 |
| CH2 | The Chair | 8:30–15:30 |
| CH3 | One Used Van | 15:30–22:30 |
| CH4 | The Luxury Bet | 22:30–30:00 |
| CH5 | The Ghost and the Bill | 30:00–38:00 |
| CH6 | Still at the Chair | 38:00–45:00 |

### S0-D. Risk scan

| # | Risk | Exposure | How the film handles it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Living subject (6.4) | Ramesh Babu, a Bengaluru businessman who has given many interviews. Under US law he is probably a limited-purpose public figure, but that is a legal judgment and needs a lawyer's review. India's defamation law also applies where he lives (Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita s.356, which replaced IPC ss.499–500 in July 2024; verify). Research found no court case, regulator action or consumer dispute. | No allegation of any kind. Every figure is attributed. "Billionaire barber" appears only as a media nickname, attributed. The ₹1,200 crore net-worth figure has no filing behind it, so it is left out, or described as a figure we could not trace to any filing. No false light and no implied endorsement. The description states: "Not affiliated with or endorsed by Ramesh Babu or Ramesh Tours and Travels." See LEAD-01. |
| 2 | Mistaken identity | Other public figures share the name: Yusuf Shariff ("KGF Babu", Bengaluru, fined for unpaid road tax on Rolls-Royces in 2025), the Telugu actor-producer Ghattamaneni Ramesh Babu (died 2022), and Karnataka MLC Ramesh Babu | Nothing about them enters the film. S2 checks that each source names the barber. The registry listing for "Ramesh Babu Gopal" matches him by name only, so the film cites the company record, not that director listing. |
| 3 | Existing company | Ramesh Tours and Travels Pvt Ltd, CIN U63040KA2004PTC033737, incorporated 12 April 2004, listed as Active by registry aggregators | Only public-record facts. No company logo. Review-site ratings are not used as claims. |
| 4 | Private individuals | His mother, grandmother, siblings and uncle (none named in the sources), his wife (not named), and the woman his mother worked for (named in one 2018 report) | No names, faces or addresses. The arrangement over the father's shop is told as "reports differ", so no unnamed relative is cast as a villain. The woman is described only by her role. His wife appears only through "his wife's jewelry", as he described it. |
| 5 | Minors | One profile says he has two children; names and ages unknown | Never named, shown or discussed. His own childhood is shown through objects, never through a stock child standing in for him. |
| 6 | Sensitive material (6.5, 10.12) | His father's death (cause unknown; "heart attack" appears only in low-quality blogs), childhood hunger ("one meal a day", attributed), debt | The cause of death is never stated. No identifiable stock face appears over hardship, death or debt: hands, empty rooms and objects instead. No poverty shown for shock. No exotic clichés (snake charmers, slums, sitar stings) and no accent humor. Not told as an immigrant story: he built his business in Bengaluru. |
| 7 | Brands | Rolls-Royce, Maruti, Mercedes-Benz, Maybach, Land Rover, BMW, Intel, Pilot | Editorial documentary context with no endorsement. Emblems may appear inside the film as part of the story but never in thumbnails. No brand-style typography. Intel appears by name only. A stock car is never presented as his car; it is labeled "Illustrative". |
| 8 | Stock people (production rule 3) | Pexels and Pixabay forbid showing identifiable people in a bad light or implying endorsement, and there are no model releases | Stock people appear only as anonymous city life, never as Ramesh, his father, mother, uncle, wife or children, and never over negative narration. Readable license plates on stock cars are blurred, which also stops a random car reading as part of his fleet. The rural-market barber in Pexels 34445702 is identifiable, so that clip is used only as neutral trade context, cropped to the hands. |
| 9 | Free-stock license limits | Each site's license differs | **Videvo:** Free-license clips that allow commercial use only. Never Editorial Use Only, Premium or attribution-unclear clips. A CC BY 3.0 clip is used only if its page names the author; it is then credited in the end card and the description, marked "modified". **Coverr:** downloaded by hand, never through an API. **Mixkit:** Free License items only; skip anything marked Restricted. **Pexels and Pixabay:** never redistribute unaltered files. A license record is kept for every clip. |
| 10 | Music and Content ID | Some Pixabay tracks are registered in Content ID by their uploaders | Choose tracks with no Content ID registration. The editor checks every track page for a registration notice and keeps a saved PDF of the page plus the download record (date, URL, license). Mixkit music is used only under its free license terms. Every cue is fingerprinted before release. The film and its music are never enrolled in Content ID. |
| 11 | Narration audio | AI audio is banned | Human voice actor with a signed release |
| 12 | Strength of the fact base | Every fact comes from search-engine excerpts; no article was read in full; every quote is unverified | Quotes are paraphrased and attributed ("he told YourStory in 2014 that…"). S2 verifies each claim (LEAD-02). |
| 13 | Conflicting facts (production rule 7) | Many reports disagree | **Birth year:** never stated; "at seven" when his father died (the age nearly every source gives). **Father's death:** 1979 or 1989 → "when he was seven", with "reports differ" if a year is shown. **Salon takeover:** 1989, 1991, or "in the early 1990s". **First van:** "accounts range from 1993 to 1995" (one outlet dates the taxi business to 2004). **The shop arrangement:** five rupees a day or a month, paid by the uncle or from a lease → "reports differ". **Ghost:** 2010 or 2011; price ₹3–4 crore (one outlier says ₹1.2 crore) → a range with the outlets named. **Road tax:** "over ₹3 crore" or ₹3.78 crore. **Jewelry:** mortgaged or sold. **Fleet:** 67 vehicles (Dec 2011) up to 600 (about 2019), and counts mix vans and minibuses with luxury cars → an on-screen chart, "What outlets reported, by year". **Mother's work:** "worked in other people's homes". **Haircut price:** ₹65–150, each with its date. **Employees:** 120 or 300. **Intel as first client:** attributed. **Celebrity clients:** "outlets have reported film-star clients; none confirmed", with no names. **Singapore training:** left out. |
| 14 | Currency conversion | The research found no exchange rates | Convert each rupee amount at the average INR/USD rate for its own year (FRED AEXINUS; verify), then adjust to 2026 dollars with CPI-U. Show both "then" and "today" values. One method card appears on screen. Pre-1992 rates moved sharply around the 1991 devaluation, so each conversion uses the exact year. GAP until S2. |
| 15 | Platform policy | YouTube rules on reused content and misleading packaging | Original narration, analysis and graphics run throughout. "Billionaire" is never asserted. With no AI and no altered real footage, no synthetic-content disclosure is expected (confirm at upload). The description carries "Educational; not financial advice." |
| 16 | Thumbnail source | No subject imagery available | Stock objects (R3) plus typography (R1). No faces, no logos, no Rolls-Royce grille, badge or mascot. Every image in a thumbnail also appears in the film. |
| 17 | Accessibility | Long film with fast cuts | Subtitles, 4.5:1 text contrast, no more than 3 flashes per second in speed ramps |

### S0-E. Leads

**R8 license requests: none.** Production rule 2 limits the ladder to R1 and R3, so personal photos or creator footage cannot be used even if offered.

Verification leads:
- **LEAD-01:** A courtesy and fact-check note to Ramesh Tours and Travels through its published contact channel. It asks them to confirm the van year, the salon takeover year, the Ghost's year and price, the road-tax figure and today's fleet size. Any reply is logged with its date. No footage or photos are requested.
- **LEAD-02:** Open the full articles and check every paraphrase before the S5 script is locked: YourStory (25 Apr 2014), Onmanorama (30 Jul 2018), Deccan Chronicle (27 May 2015 and 13 Feb 2016), The Better India (/90491), India TV (/3866), Cartoq (2024–25), Business Today (9 Oct 2025).
- **LEAD-03:** Conversion data: FRED AEXINUS (annual) and DEXINUS (daily), BLS CPI-U (CPIAUCSL), and the RBI reference rate as a cross-check.
- **LEAD-04:** The Ministry of Corporate Affairs master record for CIN U63040KA2004PTC033737.
- **LEAD-05:** View counts for the teardown (queries in 5.0).

### 5.1 Topic score

| Criterion | The usual framing ("barber who owns a Rolls-Royce") |
|---|---|
| 1. Recognizability | 5: every US viewer knows Rolls-Royce; searches for his name come mostly from India (unverified) |
| 2. Hidden angle | 5: this framing is widely covered |
| 3. Emotional engine | 9: underdog/reversal first, money second |
| 4. Now-peg | 5: the latest reported fleet addition is a Range Rover in October 2025; no 2026 peg found |
| 5. Visual provability | 5: no footage of the subject; R1 and R3 only |
| 6. Format gap | 7: research found no long-form English documentary (verify) |
| 7. Policy safety | 9: no allegations; brands appear in editorial context; minors excluded |
| **Total** | **45/70**, below 50, so three sharper angles: |

| Angle | Scores 1–7 | Total |
|---|---|---|
| **A1 "The Rolls-Royce math":** how the money actually moved, from a few rupees a day to a used-van loan to a Rolls-Royce and a tax bill that nearly sank him, set against the growing numbers attached to him online | 6, 8, 9, 5, 7, 7, 9 | **51** |
| A2 "The chair he never gave up": why the owner of a fleet still cuts hair | 5, 7, 8, 5, 6, 7, 9 | 47 |
| A3 "One pair of hands, two businesses" | 5, 6, 7, 5, 6, 6, 9 | 44 |

**Chosen: A1, with A2's question as the film-level loop.** A money ledger can be proven honestly with R1 graphics, and sorting out his numbers is an angle none of the listed videos takes.

### 5.2 Angle statement

- **Angle:** Most retellings stop at "barber buys a Rolls-Royce." This film follows the money, putting every loan, client and tax bill on one sourced ledger. It also shows where the record is solid and where the headlines outgrew it.
- **Central question:** How does a man get from one used van to a Rolls-Royce without ever putting down the scissors?
- **Why now:** The reported fleet is still growing; Business Today and Daily Jagran reported a Range Rover added in October 2025. So are the numbers attached to him online, from 67 cars to 600, plus a "billionaire" label that no filing supports. The film sorts out the record.
- **Who it is for:** US viewers who follow self-made business stories, car culture and money breakdowns.
- **What the viewer feels:**
  - Minute 1: curiosity fixed on a contradiction: a Rolls-Royce and a barber's chair, one man.
  - Midpoint: tension, as ordinary rentals fill with competitors and he bets on luxury in 2004.
  - End: earned respect and one practical takeaway: he kept the trade that started everything.

Every title has 3–8 words and at most one all-caps word. None uses an accusatory word about a named subject, and none uses V1's wording.

**Reasoning for the best three**
- **A, "Why a Rolls-Royce Owner Still Cuts Hair":** the strongest curiosity gap for US viewers who don't know his name. The answer is held back until CH6 and paid off with his own stated reasons, paraphrased and attributed. The contradiction can be shown honestly with objects. Risk: the title is in the present tense. If S2 cannot confirm he was cutting hair in 2025, it becomes "Why a Rolls-Royce Owner Kept Cutting Hair."
- **B, "How Did a Barber Pay for a Rolls-Royce?":** matches the chosen angle (the money ledger) directly. The answer lands in CH5 with dollar conversions and sourced ranges, and it draws a money-minded audience in Test & Compare.
- **C, "One Rolls-Royce Day Equals How Many Haircuts?":** a concrete sum built from two documented rates. The answer is shown as a range (500–750) with both outlets named, which also delivers BTB-1. Risk: the rates are older, so S2 must date them on screen.

### 5.4 Thumbnail briefs

Rules for all three: 1280×720, readable at 168×94 px, no faces of real people, no logos, no Rolls-Royce grille, badge or mascot, no Intel or Maruti marks. Every image also appears in the film. Text uses 0–3 words that add to the title. No AI mark is needed because nothing is AI-made.

**TH-A (title A)**
- **Main subject:** open steel barber scissors (or a straight razor) on a worn wooden counter, in hard tungsten side light with a chrome glint.
- **Contrast cue:** scale contrast. Behind it, out of focus through a shop window, the long dark side of a luxury sedan with a chrome door handle (no badge, grille or mascot in frame). A thin red arrow points to the scissors.
- **Words:** "HE KEPT THIS"
- **Color and emotion:** amber blade against teal-black; intrigue.
- **Source (R3 composite the editor builds):** the hand and blade cropped from Pexels 7697526 "Man Holding a Straight Razor", or a Mixkit barber-category clip with no face in frame; the sedan side from Pexels 9997960 "Silver Luxury Car in Car Dealership" (8K), regraded dark with the badge out of frame.
- **Fallback (R1):** the editor's own line drawings of scissors and a sedan silhouette on a teal-black gradient, with the same words.

**TH-B (title B)**
- **Main subject:** a hand-ruled ledger seen from above, with a column of handwritten entries, a pen across it and warm light. No face.
- **Contrast cue:** one blank line circled in thick red marker.
- **Words:** "PLUS TAX" (points to the road-tax twist without giving it away)
- **Color and emotion:** cream paper, black ink, red marker; urgency.
- **Source (R3):** Pexels, Pixabay or Mixkit, query "ledger book handwriting pen top view".
- **Fallback (R1):** the editor-built ledger card from the film's progress motif (generic paper texture, not a replica of any real document), with the same red circle.

**TH-C (title C)**
- **Main subject:** a two-up split. Left: an empty vintage barber chair (chrome footrest, worn leather) under a single bulb. Right: black car paint and a chrome door handle with reflections, no badge.
- **Contrast cue:** a large white "=" between the halves and a "?".
- **Words:** "1 DAY ="
- **Color and emotion:** warm leather against cold black chrome; curiosity.
- **Source (R3):** the chair from Pixabay, Mixkit or Coverr ("empty barber chair vintage"); the car detail from Pixabay or Videvo Free ("black car door handle close").
- **Fallback (R1):** the editor's vector chair and sedan icons with the "=" sign.

### 5.5 Title contract (title A + TH-A; B and C are added for Test & Compare)

| # | Promise | Planned payoff | Chapter, % of runtime |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | He owns a Rolls-Royce: a Ghost bought in 2011 (one report says 2010) | Proof beat at 0:30–1:15, full story later | CH0 about 2%; CH5 about 70% |
| P2 | He still cuts hair, as of the latest reports (2024–2025) | Proof beat, then a full scene at the chair | CH0 about 2%; CH6 about 88% |
| P3 | The answer to "why": his own stated reasons, paraphrased with attribution (India TV; Deccan Chronicle, 2016) | Final payoff, held back until then | CH6 about 96% |
| P4 | "HE KEPT THIS": the chair and the scissors as the thing he never gave up | Where the chair came from, then the callback | CH2 about 22%; CH6 about 96% |

| B1 | How the car was paid for: loans, borrowing, property papers, his wife's jewelry (mortgaged or sold; reports differ) | Ledger entry 6 | CH5 about 75% |
| B2 | "PLUS TAX": the road-tax bill, shown as a range | 80% cliffhanger | CH5 about 80% |
| C1 | How many haircuts one Rolls-Royce day equals: 500–750 at the rates two outlets reported | Kinetic sum on screen | CH5 about 83% |

S3 must tie every promise to an SC ID and an h:mm:ss timestamp. A promise left unbound is a CRITICAL failure.

Runtime 45:00 · American English · Standard mode, delivered in one pass · Stage S2

## S2: Claim ledger

**How to read the ledger**
- **Language:** the client language is American English, so the engine's two wording columns (client language and English) are one column here.
- **Batch size:** production rule 1 asks for the whole film in one table, which overrides the engine's 25-claim batch limit.
- **Order:** claims follow their planned first use, chapter by chapter (CH0–CH6 from the S0-C skeleton). S3 and S5 bind each claim to SC IDs.
- **Confidence:** every source is a search-engine excerpt, and no article was read in full. Confidence is therefore capped at **medium**, with one exception: the company registry record (C33), which three registry sites agree on. LEAD-02 (read every article in full) can raise a claim's confidence. It can never lower a wording rule below what this table allows.
- **Money (version lock):**
  - Every rupee figure appears on screen in **US dollars** as the headline number. The rupee original is shown small, with a chip giving the year-matched rate.
  - The research gives no exchange rates and no CPI values. Every dollar value is therefore **PENDING (LEAD-03)**. Each ESTIMATE row below states its rupee amount, year, rate series and formula, so the number can be filled in without new judgment. No number is guessed.
  - Rupee amounts in digits: 1 lakh = ₹100,000; 1 crore = ₹10,000,000.
- **Quotes:** none is verified word for word. Every QUOTE row is a **paraphrase with attribution** ("he told YourStory in 2014 that…") and never appears as a verbatim quote on screen.
- **Wording rule:** "Wording rule" is what narration and on-screen text may say. Anything the rule does not allow is out of the film.

# Ramesh Babu: From Barber to Rolls-Royce
Runtime 45:00 · American English · Standard mode, delivered in one pass · Stage S3

---

## S3: Story architecture and retention map

### S3-0. How to read this stage

- **Chapter IDs:** the film has exactly six chapters, **C1–C6**. Claim IDs always have two digits (C01–C73), so "C1" is a chapter and "C01" is a claim. To avoid a third meaning, the S1 title-contract promise "C1" is renamed **TC1** here.
- **Mapping from the S0-C skeleton:** CH0 (cold open) and CH1 merge into **C1**. CH2 becomes C2, CH3 becomes C3, CH4 becomes C4, CH5 becomes C5 and CH6 becomes C6. Every S2 "Used in" entry follows this mapping, so CH0 becomes C1.
- **Scene clock:** 270 scenes, SC0001–SC0270, with whole-second nominal starts. The timestamps in sections C, D and E are locked anchors. S5 may move a scene boundary by up to ±2 s, but it may not move an anchored beat, and each segment must still end on its stated time.
- **Narration pace:** 150 wpm (2.5 words/s). The narrated words come to 6,210, and the 22 wordless scenes total 216 s, which matches S0-B.
- **AI reconstruction:** 0 s in every chapter (production rule 2). Every visual event is R1 (a graphic the editor builds) or R3 (Pexels, Pixabay, Videvo Free license, Coverr downloaded by hand, Mixkit Free License).

### A. Arc and logline

**Arc:** Success story (origin → the bet → near-failure → breakthrough → cost → transferable lesson). It opens cold on a defining contradiction, the way the biography arc does. A money ledger is the film's spine, borrowed from the money-and-scale arc's withheld-answer ladder: six sourced entries, and the full ledger is withheld until 37:50.

**Logline:** His father died when he was seven. His family's share of the barbershop was five rupees. From there, a Bengaluru barber turned one used van into a luxury rental fleet and, by most accounts, bought a Rolls-Royce Ghost in 2011. By his own account, the road tax that followed almost bankrupted him. As of the latest reports, he still cuts hair. This film follows the money, one sourced entry at a time.

**Central question (said aloud in B4):** How does a man get from one used van to a Rolls-Royce without ever putting down the scissors?

There are 22 wordless scenes (216 s): C1 4, C2 3, C3 3, C4 4, C5 5, C6 3. "Wordless" means no narration; ambience and SFX always run, and music runs unless the brief marks the scene as silence.

**Visual events per chapter (total 475; R1 190 / R3 285):** C1 95 (R1 38 / R3 57), C2 70 (26 / 44), C3 72 (30 / 42), C4 78 (34 / 44), C5 85 (34 / 51), C6 75 (28 / 47). The hook carries 11 cuts in 30 s, about one every 2.7 s.
**R1 builds by chapter (95 unique):** C1 20, C2 12, C3 14, C4 17, C5 16, C6 16.
**Stock rule:** every chapter is shorter than 10 minutes, so no stock clip appears twice inside one chapter. A clip may return in a later chapter only if it starts at least 10:00 after its last use, at most 3 times in the film, and the new use differs by at least 50% of the frame area. The 148-clip pool is introduced front-loaded, and the S5 source log enforces the spacing.

### B3. But/therefore spine (no "and then")

His father dies when he is seven, **but** the shop stays open → **therefore** the family lives on a five-rupee share and his mother works in other people's homes → **therefore** he works from about thirteen → **but** around 1990 he takes the shop back → **therefore** he runs the salon by day and studies at night → **but** a used van bought on a loan nearly sinks him → **therefore**, on a suggestion from the woman his mother worked for, he rents it out, and Intel (as outlets report) becomes the first client → **therefore**, by 2004, he has about seven cars → **but** ordinary rental is getting crowded (as he tells it) → **therefore** he incorporates and buys a luxury car → **therefore**, in 2011 by most accounts, he buys a Rolls-Royce Ghost → **but** the road tax nearly bankrupts him → **therefore** he gives up other property and keeps the cars → **but** through all of it, he keeps the chair → **therefore** the final question is why.

Spacing rule: no two INTs closer than 15 s, and no gap between them longer than 35 s. The hook's 11 cuts are hook density and are not counted as INTs. A ledger appearance at a reset belongs to the reset, not to the INT count. A ledger appearance between resets (for example, a margin tick) may count as a visual-mode INT. Flashes in speed ramps stay at 3 per second or fewer.

### C3. Progress motif: the Ledger (FX-COUNTER)

A hand-ruled ledger page built in R1: cream paper texture (generic, not a replica of any real document), black ink, one red marker accent. It has six numbered lines and a left margin that carries the current age or year. Every entry lands with a pen-scratch SFX and shows its claim ID chip in the scene (BTB-3). It appears at every reset for 2–3 s. Each appearance restates the build with new data, which counts as an R1 restate and is not a repeat.

### F. Emotion curve

- **C1 The Shop:** a jolt of curiosity (the contradiction), then a grief that is held back, never milked, then a quiet, practical resolve. The low point is SC0021 (wordless, empty shop).
- **C2 The Chair:** reclaiming. Steady pride in craft and discipline, warm and unhurried.
- **C3 One Used Van:** worry (a loan he can't keep up with), then relief (the rental idea), then momentum (the first client, seven cars).
- **C4 The Luxury Bet:** tension at the turn, then confidence as the company becomes official and the first luxury car arrives. The curve rises.
- **C5 The Ghost and the Bill:** awe, then creeping dread, then the low point of the film (36:30, "almost bankrupt"), then relief (he kept the cars), then a wry, delighted beat (500–750 haircuts).
- **C6 Still at the Chair:** clarity (what the record does and doesn't support), then earned respect, then warmth at the payoff. The close is reflective and lands on the present.

### F2. Music arc

Sources: Pixabay Music and Mixkit free music only. On every Pixabay track page the editor checks for a Content ID registration notice and prefers tracks with none, saves the page as PDF, and logs the URL, download date and license. Mixkit tracks are used under its free license only. Every cue, and the final mix, is fingerprinted before release. The S5 cue sheet records final track IDs. Search terms are in English. Music cues change on story beats, never on a timer. One recurring theme (a four-note solo cello motif) appears at the proof and returns for the payoff, with variations in between. No exotic stings, no sitar, no "inspirational" piano-and-claps.

| M02 | 0:00:30–0:01:15 | SC0005–SC0010 | PROOF + title | Solo cello theme over a soft pulse, swelling to a hit on the title card | "emotional cello solo cinematic", "documentary cello theme" | Ring-out under SC0011 (J-cut) |

| M04 | 0:03:45–0:06:15 | RS02 | Five rupees | Restrained plucked strings, clock-like pizzicato synced to ledger ticks, 84 BPM | "pizzicato minimal thoughtful", "ticking clock documentary" | Holds through wordless SC0040, crossfades |

| M08 | 0:15:30–0:19:00 | RS06 | Van and loan | Road pulse, muted electric guitar and bass, minor key under the loan beat, 100 BPM | "road trip pulse muted guitar", "night drive ambient bass" | Dips at about 0:17:50 for the SC0110 road hold |
| M09 | 0:19:00–0:22:28 | RS07 | Rental idea, first client | Building optimism, strings and light percussion, with a major lift on SC0124, 104 BPM | "uplifting strings build documentary" (no claps) | **Hard cut to silence at 0:22:28** |

Sound beds: a continuous ambience bed rebuilt from Pixabay and Mixkit SFX (shop room tone, street wash, rain, engine idle), so no gap falls to digital silence. Pen scratch on ledger entries, a tick on map pins, a paper rustle on R1 document cards, a steel ring on blades. Loudness targets: about −14 LUFS integrated and −1 dBTP.

### H. Chapter briefs (each one stands alone for a separate S5 writer)

**Rules for every chapter:**
- **Depiction:**
  - No stock face is ever presented as Ramesh Babu, his father, mother, uncle, wife or children.
  - He appears only as hands, the back of a head, a silhouette, POV or objects (scissors, straight razor, steering wheel, keys, ledger).
  - Stock people appear only as anonymous city life, and never over negative narration.
  - Children are never named, shown or mentioned.
  - The cause of his father's death is never stated.
- **Cars and places:**
  - Readable license plates are blurred.
  - Every stock car under narration about his cars is labeled "Illustrative".
  - Modern footage is never passed off as the 1980s or 1990s. Period years are shown only in R1 typography.
- **Wording and figures:**
  - Every number carries a source chip (BTB-1), and every contested figure appears as a range or a "reports differ" line.
  - Every rupee figure leads in dollars (C10 method), with the rupees shown small (BTB-2). Dollar values stay "[C##]" placeholders until LEAD-03 fills them.
  - Quotes are paraphrased and attributed, never typed as verbatim quotation cards.
  - Banned list: no "you won't believe", "ever wondered", "buckle up", "stay till the end", or empty "amazing / unbelievable / secrets".
- **Finish:** LOOK-M plus the chapter trim; one grain setting; up to 1.15× push-ins on 4K sources (deeper only on 5K or larger); 1080p sources only for wide shots or a half-frame split.

**PROOF, SC0005–SC0010 (0:30–1:15):**
- **SC0005:** Name card in typography only, over Pexels 28837593 (Bengaluru timelapse): "Ramesh Babu · founder, Ramesh Tours and Travels · Bengaluru (formerly Bangalore)". (C01)
- **SC0006:** Compact method chip, then the line "Dollar figures here: rupees at that year's exchange rate, adjusted to today's money." (C10; the full card follows at SC0026)

**RS01, SC0011–SC0025 (1:15–3:45), "Brigade Road." Ledger margin: "AGE 7".**

**Hands to C2:** the open question of whose shop it is (L04), the Ledger at E1, margin "ABOUT 13".

A few years earlier, he'd made a bet that had nothing to do with hair." **Opens L05.** It keeps L01 alive through the image of the chair.

**Fact density:** C2 rests on four core claims (C20, C22, C23, C24). Do not pad with invented scenes or feelings. Use the craft montage, honest "the record doesn't say" beats, and emotional holds of up to 12 s.
**Stock pools:** barber chair and mirror details, strop, towel steam, scissors and comb macro (Mixkit barber category, past the first screen; Pixabay sorted by newest). Pexels 34445702 (an identifiable rural barber) is used only cropped to the hands, as neutral trade context.

# Ramesh Babu: From Barber to Rolls-Royce
Runtime 45:00 · American English · Standard mode, delivered in one pass · Stage S4

---

## S4: Production bibles

### S4-0. How to read this stage, and what overrides the engine

- **Every token here is a selection-and-finish spec, not an AI prompt.** The AI reconstruction cap is 0 s (production rule 2), so no AI prompt exists. Each token's **consistency line** (in English) is pasted verbatim into every S5 shot line that uses it. It tells the editor what to pick from Pexels, Pixabay, Videvo (Free license), Coverr (downloaded by hand) or Mixkit (Free License), and how to finish it.
- **Overrides of engine section 8** (the production rules win):
  1. Grain goes on everything, archive or not, with one grain setting for the whole film (rule 5). The engine's "grain on genuine archive only" does not apply, because this film has no archive.
  2. Audio comes only from Pixabay Music, Pixabay sound effects, Mixkit free music and Mixkit free SFX. The engine's Freesound, SFX-library and generator routes are off. No AI-generated audio of any kind.
  3. **Narration is a human recording.** Rule 2 bans AI-generated audio, so a TTS voice is used only as a **scratch track for timing** and never reaches the release (8.1).
  4. The master runs at 23.976 fps, not the engine's 25 fps, for cinema motion cadence (8.4-C).
  5. There is no face reference pack and no face description for any real person. No face of a real person ever appears in this film, and no stock face stands in for one.
- **New token:** LOOK-D (the cold drift, 0:33:00–0:36:50), which S3's C5 brief describes but did not name. LOOK-G is the finishing pass for graphics.
- **Correction to S3:** the plan for SC0070 says "HERO-RAZOR, overhead, with the strop." One clip cannot change its camera angle. HERO-RAZOR is therefore picked as a **set**: two or more clips from the same contributor and the same shoot, one of them overhead (OBJ-01). If no such set exists, SC0070 uses a different-region L5 crop of the hero clip, and S5 drops the word "overhead."

---

### 8.1 Narrator: SND-VO

| Field | Spec |
|---|---|
| Voice | Male, apparent age 40–55, General American accent. A warm, low-mid baritone with a little texture. Not a movie-trailer voice, not a motivational-speaker cadence, not a newsreader's. |
| Persona | A calm, curious, observational guide who has read every source and trusts the facts to carry the drama. Direct, personal American documentary storytelling: short declarative sentences, concrete nouns, momentum from "but" and "therefore." |
| Pace | **150 wpm average (2.5 words/s)**, matching S3: 6,210 words over 2,484 narrated seconds. By section: hook 150–158 wpm; information beats 150–155; grief and emotional holds (C1 RS01, the C5 cliff, the C6 payoff) 135–140 with longer pauses. Any section read slower is balanced by **tightening text, never by speeding the read**. |
| Emotional range | Restrained. The father's death (C1) is read plainly with no tremble. The tax bill (C5) is read slower and lower, with no alarm. The haircut math (SC0225) gets a dry half-smile. The payoff (C6) has quiet warmth. The narrator never shouts, never sells and never says a number with awe. |
| "We" and "you" | "We" only for the film's own research ("No filing we could find backs that up"), never for travel or access ("we went to Bengaluru" is banned). "You" at most once per chapter, and only for the viewer's stake (hook B4). |
| His words | Every paraphrase is read in the narrator's own voice, framed as "He told YourStory in 2014 that…". Never an imitation of the subject, never an accent, never a "quote … unquote" delivery. |
| Comparisons | An American yardstick (a price, a wage, a car the viewer knows) is used only when the S2 ledger carries a dated number for it (LEAD-03, C10). Until then there is no yardstick; the line stands on its own number. |

**Number-reading rules (the TTS script and the human script spell every number as spoken words; the screen shows numerals):**
- **Years:** 1989 → "nineteen eighty-nine"; 1993–95 → "nineteen ninety-three to ninety-five"; 2004 → "two thousand four"; 2011 → "twenty eleven"; 2025 → "twenty twenty-five".
- **Dollars lead:** "about [X] dollars," with no "and" inside numbers (American style: "seven hundred fifty").
- **Rupees:** spoken only where S3 writes them. "Five rupees" (SC0030); "three to three point three crore rupees" (SC0192).
- **Crore:** the **first spoken "crore" (SC0192)** adds once: "a crore is ten million rupees." This is arithmetic, not a claim. The words "lakh" and "crore" are never spoken without that gloss having come first.
- **Ranges:** "five hundred to seven hundred fifty haircuts."
- **Ages:** "seven," "about thirteen." His father's death never gets a year.
- **Never spoken:** the CIN code, "Pvt Ltd" (say "Private Limited" only if it must be said), "Rs" (always "rupees"), claim IDs, "[C##]" placeholders.

**Release recording (the human narrator):**
- **Rights:** an original recording the production owns. The narrator is the producer, or a voice actor who signs a recording release: worldwide, perpetual, all media, edits and dubbing allowed, and no cloning or AI training on the voice without separate written consent.
- **Casting:** no celebrity sound-alike, and no voice that resembles Ramesh Babu or any real person in the story.
- **Chain:** large-diaphragm condenser or broadcast dynamic microphone, 15–20 cm from the mouth, 15° off-axis, pop filter. Treated room (RT60 under 0.3 s, noise floor under −60 dBFS). Record 48 kHz/24-bit mono WAV, peaks around −6 dBFS.
- **Processing:** HPF at 70 Hz; −2 dB at 300 Hz; +1.5 dB at 4 kHz (wide); de-ess at 6–8 kHz; compression 2.5:1, 3–6 dB of gain reduction; no reverb (dry and close). Light de-noise only if needed.
- **Delivery:** one WAV per chapter (C1–C6) plus pickups, slated with SC ranges.

**Scratch TTS (for timing the edit and the animatic only; replaced before release):**
- **Voice:** any stock voice in the TTS tool that fits the spec above: adult male, General American, low-mid pitch. Never a cloned or custom voice of a real person.
- **Settings:** speed 1.00, adjusted in 0.02 steps until the calibration passage (SC0011–SC0025, 375 words) measures 150 ± 3 wpm; stability 0.50–0.60; similarity/clarity 0.75; style 0.10–0.20; speaker boost on.
- **SSML pauses:** sentence end 350 ms; beat change 700 ms; before a reveal 1,000 ms (the edit then sets the exact silence, 8.5-D).
- **Lock:** the release VO is recorded against the locked cut. Any scene where the human read drifts more than ±0.5 s from the scratch is re-timed in the edit, not re-paced.

---

### 8.2 Characters (CHR) and depiction policy

There is no free stock of Ramesh Babu or his family. **No stock face is ever presented as any real person in this story, and no narration line says or implies "this is him"** ("here he is," "this man," "watch him work" are all banned over stock).

| Token | Who | Type | Depiction policy | Never |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CHR-01** | Ramesh Babu, founder of Ramesh Tours and Travels, Bengaluru | Real, living | **STAND-IN-NO-FACE**: hands, back of head, far silhouette, POV, objects, places, typography (visual grammar below). Shots that could be read as literally him carry "Illustrative." | His face, profile, jawline or reflection; any face standing in for him; an invented act; his voice imitated |
| **CHR-02** | His father, reported as P. Gopal, a barber with a shop on Brigade Road (C07) | Real, deceased | **OBJECTS-ONLY**: empty chair, towel on a hook, mirror edge, rolling shutter. The name is said once, neutrally. | Any human body part standing in for him; a year or a cause of death on screen; mourning clichés (garlanded photo, funeral) |
| **CHR-03** | His mother (name unknown) | Real, private | **HANDS-AND-OBJECTS** (SC0033): working hands at household tasks, a steel plate, a cold stove. No jewelry or bangles that suggest identity. | A woman's face; a name; poverty-porn framing |
| **CHR-04** | The relative who ran the shop, and the arrangement | Real, private | **TYPE-ONLY** (SC0027): "accounts differ" typography. Not named, not shown, not cast as a villain. | A face, a name, menacing music or a sinister look on this beat |
| **CHR-05** | His wife (name unknown) | Real, private | **OBJECT-ONLY** (SC0219): a closed jewelry box at most. | A name, a woman's face or hands, jewelry being worn |
| **CHR-06** | His children | Real, possibly minors | **NEVER**: not named, shown or mentioned. | Any reference at all |
| **CHR-07** | His grandmother and siblings | Real, private | **TYPE-ONLY**: "his family" (or the C16 wording if LEAD-02 confirms it, with no count). | Faces, names, a number |
| **CHR-08** | The woman his mother worked for, who suggested renting the van (C28) | Real, private | **NEVER SHOWN**: narration only ("the woman his mother worked for"). A name appears in one report; the film does not use it. | A name, a face, invented dialogue |
| **CHR-09** | Clients: Intel (first client, as outlets report), corporate executives, film stars (categories only) | Organization; categories | **TYPE-AND-OBJECT**: "Intel" set in Inter, never its logo or typeface. Passengers appear as gloves, a rear door, the back of a seat. | Star names or star imagery; a logo; any passenger's face |
| **CHR-10** | Anonymous city life (stock people) | Illustrative | **ANONYMOUS-CROWD**: distant, in motion, never the subject of a shot for more than 2 s, never over negative narration (poverty, debt, tax, death). | Close faces under narration about him or his family; people looking into the lens |

**CHR-01 by period (no face in any period; no reference pack is assembled):**
- **C1, ages 7 to about 13:** no human body part at all. Objects only: the shop, the newspaper bundle, the milk crate, bicycle spokes at dawn, the pen. **Never a child.**
- **From about 1990 (C2) to 2004 (C4):** adult male hands at the chair; back of head in a dim shop; driver POV; hands on keys and a wheel.
- **2011 (C5):** hands, far silhouettes at night, objects (keys, papers, a closed box).
- **2011–2025 (C6):** hands at work in a present-day salon. Mature hands are fine; never visibly young.

**CHR-01 stand-in consistency line (paste verbatim):**
> "Adult male hands, warm medium-brown skin, short clean nails, no rings, no watch, no tattoos, no gloves, plain muted shirt cuff (white, pale blue or gray, no logo); face, profile and reflections never in frame; framing cut at the forearm."

**Never-drift list for CHR-01:**
- No face in any mirror. Barbershop mirrors are the main risk: check every frame, and crop, mask or reject.
- No partial profile, ear-to-jaw line or hairline in three-quarter view.
- No visible ring, watch brand, phone or bracelet.
- No gloves on his stand-in hands. Gloved clips such as Mixkit 43224 appear only as anonymous trade context.
- No smartphone in any stand-in shot before C5.
- No electric clippers with LED displays, no branded products and no printed capes in C1–C3.

**Visual grammar for CHR-01 (seven devices; S5 tags each with one):**

| Code | Device | Rule | Main chapters |
|---|---|---|---|
| G-H | Hands | At the chair, on keys, on a wheel, on papers. Cut at the forearm. Match the skin tone in the consistency line. | C2, C3, C5, C6 |
| G-B | Back of head | Only in low light, out of focus or behind the chair; no hairstyle detail held longer than 2 s | C2, C4 |
| G-S | Silhouette | Far and backlit, crushed to black, no profile edge against a bright window | C4, C5 |
| G-P | POV | Driver POV, POV over the chair, POV down at a ledger | C3, C5 |
| G-O | Objects | Razor, scissors, chair, keys, wheel, ledger, pen | All |
| G-L | Places | The shop (LOC-03), the road (LOC-05), maps (LOC-02, 04, 06) | All |
| G-T | Typography | His name card (SC0005), ledger entries, paraphrase attributions | All |

**"Illustrative" label rule:** any stock shot a viewer could read as his actual person, property or place gets the "Illustrative" label in the lower bar for the full shot. That covers hands doing what the narration says he did, every car under narration about his cars, the shop interior under "his father's shop," and any van. City texture and generic objects are not labeled. The method chip at SC0006 states once: "Footage is illustrative unless labeled otherwise."

### 8.3 Locations (LOC)

Coordinates are not given. Every map location is found by **name search in OpenStreetMap data** and checked by the editor before export. Place-name signage in stock clips is blurred or framed out when narration speaks of his shop or salon, so no real business is implied to be his.

Videvo "Majestic Bus Station" Free-license clips only, checked one by one. | Day: LOOK-B. Dawn and dusk: LOOK-C. | Metro lines and glass towers mean the present. **They never sit under a 1980s–90s line.** Hook check: if the frame under "BENGALURU, INDIA · 2011" (SC0001) shows elevated metro track or obviously recent towers, reframe to a part without them, or move the "2011" tag onto the next shot. |
| **LOC-02 BRIGADE-ROAD** | The father's shop street (by most accounts) | **Map only (FX-MAP).** Highlight the road, never a building. The "St Patrick's Complex" detail is not used. No stock footage is ever labeled Brigade Road (none exists on the five sites). | Map style (8.4-H) | No year on the map |
| **LOC-03 THE-SHOP** | The family shop, origin years (illustrative) | Stock interior **details**, never a wide room that reads as a specific real shop | LOOK-A: one bulb, sodium and tungsten warmth, deep shadows | Timeless only: worn leather, mirror edge (no faces), towel on a hook, rolling shutter, straight razor, steel comb. No LED, no brand labels, no printed capes. "Illustrative" when narration says "his father's shop." |

### 8.4 LOOK: the master grade and finish

#### A. Picture format
- **Aspect ratio:** 2.39:1 letterbox inside a 3840×2160 master. The active picture is 3840×1608 (from y = 276 to y = 1884), with black bars of 276 px top and bottom.
- **How the bars are built:** as a **matte on the top video track** (two black 3840×276 plates), not as output blanking. That way source chips and labels can sit in the lower bar.
- **Shorts and the Facebook cut:** rebuilt 9:16 or 4:5 from the full-frame sources, with no letterbox and re-set type (S3 G).
- **Color:** SDR Rec.709, gamma 2.4.

#### B. Lens classes (stock is picked by its framing)
- **WIDE** (24 mm feel): establishing shots only. 1080p sources are allowed only here or as half of a split.
- **MID** (50 mm feel): observational.
- **DETAIL** (85 mm and macro): the film's main register, because details hide stock origins best.
- **Rule:** never two identical shot sizes in a row, except a planned match cut.

#### C. Frame rate and conform
- **Master:** 23.976 fps.
- **23.976 or 24 fps sources:** used natively.
- **25 fps sources:** conformed to 23.976 (a 4% slow-down, invisible on B-roll).
- **29.97 or 30 fps sources:** conformed to 80% speed.
- **48–60 fps sources:** used for slow motion at 40–50%.
- **Retiming:** never frame blending. Use "Nearest," and optical flow (enhanced, better) only inside speed ramps.
- **Stock audio:** every stock clip is **muted** (8.5).

#### D. LOOK-M, the master grade (DaVinci Resolve; Studio features have a free-version fallback in brackets)

**Project setup:**
- DaVinci YRGB Color Managed.
- Timeline space: DaVinci Wide Gamut / DaVinci Intermediate.
- Output: Rec.709 gamma 2.4.
- Default input: Rec.709 gamma 2.4, overridden per clip. Use the camera log transform for log drone footage, and Rec.2100 HLG with tone mapping for HLG phone footage.

**Clip-level nodes (one stack per clip):**
1. **N1 Input:** a CST override if the clip is not Rec.709.
2. **N2 Noise reduction:** temporal, 2 frames, better, motion medium; luma/chroma 4/6 on UHD sources, 8/10 on 1080p sources. Spatial NR at 3–5 only if blocks show. [Free version: skip, and lean on N5's blur.]
3. **N3 Balance:** Offset for white balance, until the RGB parade aligns on a neutral surface. Mid-tones sit at about 40–45 IRE.
4. **N4 Privacy:** tracked blur on plates, legible signage, any stray face and third-party logos. Gaussian, strong enough that nothing can be read.
5. **N5 Stock-tell kill:**
   - Hue vs Sat: blues −25%, cyans −20%, greens −15%.
   - Sat vs Sat: compress input above 0.7 by 20%.
   - Lum vs Sat: −30% above 80% luma, −40% below 10% luma.
   - Blur radius 0.48 (0.50 is neutral) on 4K clips with in-camera edge sharpening. Not on 5K or 8K sources.
6. **N6 Chapter trim:** LOOK-A, B, C or D (below).

**Group post-clip "FOOTAGE" (LOOK-M):**
1. **Contrast:** 1.10 at pivot 0.40 (DI). Custom curve toe lifted to 0.03 for a soft black (blacks land at 2–4 IRE). Shoulder rolls off from 0.85 to 0.95, so speculars never clip hard.
2. **Split tone:** shadows (Lift) toward hue 200°, amount 0.04; highlights (Gain) toward hue 45°, amount 0.03; mid-tones neutral.
3. **Saturation:** 0.88 overall.
4. **Halation:**
   - Studio: the Halation OFX, threshold about 0.80, radius medium, tint red-orange, about 10% mix.
   - Free version: luma key 0.80–1.00 with softness 0.10, blur 0.7, tint hue 15°, Add mix at 10%.
   - It only reads where a highlight meets dark (chrome, bulbs, lane lines). That is intended.
5. **Glow:** threshold 0.85, spread 0.3, gain 0.10. Only in LOOK-C and LOOK-D car shots.
6. **Vignette:** soft oval fitted to 2.39, −0.15 stop at the edge.

**Timeline node (everything, footage and graphics):**
- **Film grain, one setting for the whole film:** 35 mm-style, size 0.65, strength 0.18, softness 0.25, grain saturation 0.15. Grain is set slightly coarse so it survives YouTube's re-encode.
- **Order:** grain is applied after all scaling. **Never sharpen after grain.**

**Sharpening and upscaling:**
- No sharpening in the grade.
- 1080p sources: Super Scale 2× (medium, sharpness 0.3, NR 0.3) [free version: "Sharper" scaling], only for wide shots or a half-frame split (S3).

**Push-in limits:**
- 4K sources: 1.15×.
- 5K sources: 1.30×.
- 8K sources: 1.6×.
- Eased in and out, over the full shot length.

#### E. Chapter trims (the N6 node; values relative to LOOK-M)

| Token | Use | Recipe |
|---|---|---|
| **LOOK-A** sodium-street tungsten | C1; C2 until RS05 | White balance warmer (about +300 K equivalent); Gain toward hue 38°, 0.03; saturation 0.82; black toe 0.035; contrast 1.05. Reads like memory. |
| **LOOK-B** neutral daylight | C3, C4 | Neutral white balance; saturation 0.90; contrast 1.10; crisp, never cold. C4 car shots get LOOK-C at a 30% mix (the "first gold accents"). C2 RS05 crossfades from A to B over 3:30. |
| **LOOK-C** restrained gold | C5 0:30:00–0:33:00 and 0:36:50–0:38:00; all of C6 | Gain toward hue 45°, 0.05; mid-tones +0.01 warm; saturation 0.92; glow on. Gold, never brass or orange. |
| **LOOK-D** cold drift | C5 0:33:00 → 0:36:50 | Keyframed from LOOK-C to: Lift toward hue 210°, 0.05; saturation 0.70; contrast 1.15; highlights neutral. It reaches full strength at 0:36:00 (SC0217) and eases back to LOOK-C from 0:36:50 as the theme returns. |
| **LOOK-G** graphics | Every R1 graphic | No color look, so brand colors stay exact. Gets the timeline grain, halation on elements brighter than 85%, a 2% vignette, and a 0.3 px blur so type never looks digital-crisp against grainy footage. |

#### F. Color-matching rules
1. **One reference still per chapter** (REF-C1 … REF-C6), taken from its hero shot. Every clip is matched against it with split-screen wipes and checked on scopes, not by eye alone.
2. **Match order:** black level → white balance on a neutral surface → mid-tone exposure → saturation → the key hue (sodium amber, chrome, sky).
3. **Neighbors:** adjacent shots differ by no more than 5 IRE in average luma, unless the difference is a planned cut-to-dark.
4. **Skies:** no saturated blue sky anywhere. Desaturate it to half or frame it out.
5. **Skin on stand-in hands:** sits on the vectorscope skin line, ±5°.
6. **Black paint:** true black at 0–3 IRE, with speculars rolled at 92–95.
7. **Phone-shot clips:** a clip that still looks like a phone after N5 (HDR halos, crushed micro-contrast) is dropped.

#### G. Making free stock unrecognizable
**At selection:**
- Search with 2–4 plain nouns plus one mood word.
- Go past the first two screens of results; on Pixabay, sort by newest.
- Where download counts show, prefer the less-downloaded clip, and never take the top results for generic terms ("barber," "luxury car," "money," "city").
- Reverse-image-search one frame of every hero clip and drop it if it is common in ads or thumbnails (S3 I).
- Reject anything with a watermark, burned-in text, people smiling or looking into the lens, a third-party logo outside the story's brands, or an AI-generated label or synthetic look (rule 2).

**At finishing:**
- Crop away stock's centered compositions; reframe to thirds within the 2.39 extraction.
- Never use a clip's first or last 10 frames (stabilizer warp and fades).
- Mix locked shots with the stock-typical slider moves.
- Retime to 23.976.
- Rebuild all sound (8.5).
- Apply LOOK-M, halation and grain to everything.

**Banned clichés:** money rain, handshakes, spinning globes, a man on a mountaintop, champagne, fanned cash, hooded hackers, glowing brains, and exotic India shorthand (snake charmers, slum panoramas, sitar). These rules also follow the US framing guidance: no exoticism and no poverty porn.

#### H. Motion and edit grammar
- **Push-ins:** within the limits in D. **Lateral drift:** at most 3% of frame width per shot.
- **Locked shots for weight:** the chair, the ledger, the tax bill, the jewelry box.
- **Speed ramps:** on sources of 48 fps or higher only: 100% → 40% over 6 frames, hold, → 100% over 4 frames. No more than 3 flashes per second.
- **J-cuts and L-cuts:** ambience or music leads picture by 0.5–1.5 s at every reset.
- **Holds:** emotional beats hold 1–2 s longer than information beats. Holds run up to 12 s in C2 (S3).
- **Match-cut catalog** (MC-01 and MC-02 come from S3; the rest are available to S5):
  - MC-01: comb teeth → lane lines (SC0002)
  - MC-02: scissors closing → car door closing (SC0004)
  - MC-03: razor glint → chrome-handle glint
  - MC-04: ledger pen stroke → road lane line (C3)
  - MC-05: towel steam → rain on a windshield (from the end of C2 into C3)
  - MC-06: strop pull → seat-belt pull
  - MC-07: ledger page → tax-bill page (C5)
- **FX templates in use:** FX-MAP, FX-PAPER (ledger, registry card, tax bill), FX-KINETIC, FX-SPLIT, FX-COUNTER, FX-SOURCECHIP and NONE. FX-FILM is global grain. FX-RECON and FX-ARCHIVE are not used.

---

### 8.4b Motion-graphics style guide

**Fonts (two families, both under the SIL Open Font License, from Google Fonts; keep the license text in the project):**
- **Source Serif 4:** titles, chapter cards, ledger entries, the registry card, his name.
- **Inter:** chips, labels, maps, charts, counters (tabular figures on), lower-third role lines.
- **No brand typefaces.** "Rolls-Royce," "Intel" and "Mercedes-Benz" are set in Inter.

**Color tokens:**

| Token | Hex | Use |
|---|---|---|
| INK | #141414 | Ledger ink, type on paper |
| PAPER | #EDE4D0 | Ledger and card base (generic texture, procedural or a plain stock paper texture, no letterhead) |
| RED | #B3261E | Ledger accent only: "+ TAX," "+ ROAD TAX," the red circle, the margin rule |
| AMBER | #E0A040 | Map highlights and pins only |
| GOLD | #C9A227 | C4–C6 accents; the "luxury only" chart series |
| WHITE | #F4F1EA | Type on picture |
| GRAY | #9A9A9A | Secondary type, "reports differ" headers |
| MAP-LAND, MAP-WATER, ROAD-MINOR, ROAD-MAJOR | #15181C, #1E2A33, #343941, #646A73 | Map base |

**Layout (UHD values):**
- Title-safe text runs x = 192–3648.
- Kinetic type sits inside the active picture (y 276–1884).
- In the **lower bar:** "Illustrative" left-aligned at x = 192 and the source chip right-aligned at x = 3648, both on baseline y = 2010.
- **Minimum type size 48 px.**
  - Source chips and labels: Inter Medium 52 px.
  - Ledger lines: Source Serif 4, 68 px.
  - Lower-third name: Source Serif 4 Semibold, 96 px.
  - Kinetic numbers: Inter SemiBold, 220–320 px.
  - Chapter titles: Source Serif 4, 160 px.
- **Contrast:** at least 4.5:1. Behind white type on a background brighter than 50 IRE, add a 25% black gradient scrim.

**Label styles:**
- **Source chip (BTB-1):** "yourstory.com 2014," in Inter 52 px at 80% white. A claim-ID tag follows it at 60% (BTB-3): "C11 · yourstory.com 2014."
- **Illustrative:** Inter Italic 52 px at 70% white, on for the full shot. Line drawings say "Illustration."
- **Conversion (BTB-2):** the dollar figure is large; under it, the rupees at 45% of its size with year and unit: "₹3–3.3 crore (2011)." The full method appears once at SC0006 (compact) and once at SC0026 (full).
- **Reports differ:** two columns divided by a thin rule, each value with its own chip, under the header "REPORTS DIFFER" (Inter caps, tracking +100, GRAY). Neither value is emphasized.
- **Lower third (SC0005):** a 4 px RED rule above; name in Source Serif 4 Semibold; role line in Inter; x = 192, y = 1700.

**Kinetic rules:**
- Lands between 0.3 s before and 0.5 s after its spoken word.
- 10-frame cubic ease-out in, 8-frame fade out.
- No bounce, spin or 3D flip.
- At most 6 words per card. The ledger and document cards are exempt, but hold for at least (words ÷ 3) + 1 seconds.
- Every number carries its unit and a chip. Ranges use an en dash and "about."
- **Export gate:** search every text layer for "[" so no "[C##]" placeholder ever renders.

**FX-COUNTER, the Ledger (OBJ-05):**
- **Page:** fills the active picture. PAPER base; six numbered ruled lines (INK at 25%); a red double margin rule at the left carrying the age or year.
- **How an entry lands:** a Source Serif 4 line writes itself on (a left-to-right reveal of 0.6–1.2 s, synced to SND-FX-01 pen scratch), with a 2% drift and a rack-focus from an 8 px blur to sharp.
- **Red marker:** a hand-wobbled 10 px stroke (the blank circle on E6 line 2, "+ TAX").
- **At resets:** 2–3 s.
- **The close:** a seventh line, "Next entry:", left empty.

**Other counters and charts:**
- **Car counter (C3):** an odometer roll from 1 to 7, one digit every 6 frames, reading "ABOUT 7 CARS," with SND-FX-09 ticks.
- **Fleet chart (C6):** "What outlets reported, by year." A dot plot from 2011 to 2025: hollow WHITE dots for "total vehicles," filled GOLD dots for "luxury only," each dot labeled with its value and chip. **No connecting lines, trendline or area fill**, because the points come from different outlets counting different things. Dots land one per beat with a tick.
- **Haircut-price chart:** the same dot grammar, in dollars with rupees small. Both 2017 points shown; ₹65 excluded (S3).

**FX-MAP:**
- **Data:** OpenStreetMap, exported (a Geofabrik extract or an Overpass query), styled in QGIS and rendered at 8K for push-ins. No third-party tile service unless its terms allow video export.
- **Labels:** in English.
- **Highlights:**
  - Brigade Road: an AMBER line, 12 px, with a soft glow. Never a building.
  - Area pins (Bowring Institute area, Ejipura): a soft AMBER circle at 40% fill.
- **Moves:** at most three per map, 2–3 s each, eased.
- **Credit:** "© OpenStreetMap contributors" in the lower bar for the whole time the map is on screen.

**Document-style cards:**
- **Registry card (SC0140):** a lighter PAPER texture. Only the S3 fields; no seal, logo, address or signature. Labeled "Graphic · rebuilt from public registry data," with chip "Registry (MCA) 2004."
- **Tax bill (SC0217):** a generic bill shape with no emblem and no form numbering, reading "ROAD TAX · 2011 · more than $[C44 low]" (the dollar value stays a placeholder until LEAD-03), chips "YourStory · Deccan Chronicle."

**Cards:**
- **Title card (SC0010):** "RAMESH BABU · FROM BARBER TO ROLLS-ROYCE," Source Serif 4 Semibold caps, tracking +80, on the M02 hit.
- **Chapter cards:** "CHAPTER 2" in Inter 48 px, GRAY, tracking +200, over the title in Source Serif 4. 3–4 s with SND-DES-03.
- **End card (SC0270):** the S3 text. On-screen credits are those the licenses require (OSM; any Videvo CC BY items with author, title, link, license and "modified"). Courtesy credits for every clip and track go in the description.

**Accessibility:** no flashing above 3 per second; subtitles match narration word for word, at most two lines.

---

### 8.5 Sound bible

#### A. Buses and layers
| Bus | Content | Level under VO | Level in wordless holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| VO | SND-VO | Short-term about −15 to −14 LUFS | n/a |
| MUS | M01–M17 | −26 to −28 LUFS short-term (12–14 dB under VO); a 2–3 dB EQ dip at 2–4 kHz | Up to −16 LUFS |
| AMB | Ambience beds | −32 to −38 LUFS | −24 to −28 LUFS |
| FX | Spot effects | Peaks −12 to −6 dBFS | Same |
| DES | Risers, hits, subs, swishes | Peaks no higher than −3 dBFS; subs at 35–60 Hz | Same |
| Master | Program | **About −14 LUFS integrated, −1 dBTP** (S3) | Limiter on the master |

Music ducks 6–9 dB under VO (150 ms attack, 400 ms release), with manual automation in holds. Every other bus is high-passed at 30 Hz.

#### B. Sourcing rules (CRITICAL)
- **Allowed sources:** Pixabay sound effects and Mixkit free SFX only.
- **Stock clip audio:** every stock clip's own audio is muted, because it may hide radios, phones, music or speech. The ambience is rebuilt from SFX.
- **Not used:**
  - SFX labeled or described as AI-generated.
  - Intelligible speech in any language; walla (crowd murmur) is fine.
  - Religious sounds (prayer calls, temple bells, chanting), to keep the film neutral.
  - Exotic stings.
- **Logs:** each file's URL, title, uploader, license and download date are logged, and each page is saved as a PDF.

#### C. Tokens (SND) and search phrases (English, used as typed)

**Placement rules:**
- **Risers** end on the reveal word, not after it. They go into SC0010, SC0095, SC0124 (E3), SC0140 (E4), SC0204 (E6 line 1) and SC0228 (the full ledger). **Never into SC0217**: silence does that job.
- **Big hits:** outside the hook, at most one per minute.
- **Swishes:** at most one per 20 s.

#### D. Silence protocol (SND-SIL)
- **SIL-1, music out:** ambience stays. Used at SC0021, SC0136 (low), and 0:42:50–0:43:00 (salon ambience only).
- **SIL-2, near-silence:** VO, music and FX out; room tone at about −50 dBFS RMS, **never digital zero**. Used at 0:00:29 (1 s), 0:22:28–0:22:30, 0:35:57–0:36:00, and SC0221 (two beats, then a low drone; the theme returns at 0:36:50).
- **SIL-3, reveal breath:** voice and music drop for 1–2 beats before a key reveal, and the reveal lands on the next word. Used at the 0.5 s cut to black in the hook, at SC0124 and at SC0260.

#### E. Music plan

**Source rules (CRITICAL):**
- Pixabay Music and Mixkit free music only.
- No commercial songs, no covers, no AI-generated tracks (skip any track labeled or described as AI-made).
- No track chosen by a famous artist's name, and no sound-alike searches.
- No sitar, no "inspirational" piano-and-claps.

**Theme strategy:**
- **THEME-1** is one Pixabay Music track of at least 2:30 with an exposed solo-cello opening of at least 20 s and a later full-ensemble section.
- M02 is cut from its intro. M17 runs from the intro into the ensemble. M11 uses its ensemble section if that fits the beat.
- M06 uses a track by the same uploader in the same or a related key if one exists. If not, M06 stands alone, and the theme link rests on M02, M11 and M17.
- Every cue's key and BPM are logged (detected with a key-finder tool), and adjacent cues stay within a fifth of each other.
- Edits fall on downbeats. Hits are placed on reveal words.

**License and Content ID procedure (for every track and every SFX file):**
1. Open the item's own page. Confirm it is under the Pixabay Content License or the Mixkit free license, as that page states on the day of download.
2. On Pixabay, look for any Content ID registration notice. **Prefer tracks with none, and skip registered tracks** rather than relying on later whitelisting.
3. Skip anything labeled or described as AI-generated.
4. Save the page as a PDF. Download the license certificate if the site offers one. Log: cue ID, title, uploader, URL, download date, license name, Content ID status, duration, BPM, key.
5. Use only the original download from the site, never a re-upload elsewhere.
6. Before release, fingerprint every cue and the final mix with an audio-recognition service. Any match means the cue is swapped. Then upload privately and wait for YouTube's checks (S7).

### 8.7 Continuity rules
- **Paste tokens verbatim:** S5 lines carry token IDs, and the consistency lines live here.
- **Stand-in hands:** within one sequence, every shot of CHR-01's hands passes the same consistency line. Never cut between two visibly different pairs of hands inside one beat.
- **Cars:** one car per sequence. Never cut a silver car (OBJ-08) next to a black car (OBJ-09) or a Ghost detail (OBJ-07) as if they were the same car. **The Ghost's color is never stated** (it is not in the record).
- **Time and weather:** they change only at a scene boundary the manifest gives a reason for. Rain belongs to night road shots only.
- **Era:** C1–C3 objects stay timeless. Present-day markers (metro, glass towers, LED tools, smartphones) are allowed only in C6, and in hook shots that pass the era check in 8.3.
- **Source log:** each clip's site, ID or URL, author, license, download date, resolution, fps, page PDF, uses (SC IDs and timecodes) and L5 variant number (n/3).
- **Reuse:**
  - Any clip returns at least 10:00 after its last use, at most 3 times in the film, and the new use differs by at least 50% of the frame area (S3).
  - No clip appears twice in one chapter.
  - The hero reuse table (S3 I) is binding.

# BATCH: SC0001-SC0054 | 0:00:00-0:08:30 | Chapter C1 · The Shop (S5, compact scene lines)

**Word budget:** narration pace 150 wpm (2.5 words per second, S4 SND-VO, within the 145–155 wpm American documentary range). Narrated time is 474 s (510 s minus 36 s wordless: SC0010 7 s, SC0021 10 s, SC0040 10 s, SC0054 9 s), so the target is 474 × 2.5 = **1,185 words**. The written total and the drift are stated in the QA line at the end. Every scene stays within ±10% of its own target (scene seconds × 2.5). RS01's grief beats (SC0015–SC0020) are read at the S4 grief pace of 135–140 wpm inside the same scene lengths; the pauses come from the SSML breaks, not from extra seconds.

**Chapter at a glance:**
- **Scenes:** 54 (SC0001–SC0054), contiguous from 0:00:00 to 0:08:30, 0 s clock drift.
- **Segments:** HOOK 0:00:00–0:00:30 (SC0001–SC0004, 11 cuts) · PROOF 0:00:30–0:01:15 (SC0005–SC0010) · RS01 "Brigade Road" 0:01:15–0:03:45 (SC0011–SC0025) · RS02 "Five rupees" 0:03:45–0:06:15 (SC0026–SC0040) · RS03 "About thirteen" 0:06:15–0:08:30 (SC0041–SC0054).
- **Look:** LOOK-M + LOOK-A (sodium-street tungsten) on all footage; LOOK-G on every graphic.
- **AI:** 0 s. Every visual event is R1 (built by the editor) or R3 (Pexels, Pixabay, Videvo Free license, Coverr downloaded by hand, Mixkit Free License).
- **Loops:** opens L01 and L02 (SC0004), L03 (SC0009), L04 (SC0027). Pays none. Hands to C2: L04 open, the Ledger at E1, margin "ABOUT 13".
- **Title promises touched:** P1 proof at SC0007 (0:00:46), P2 proof at SC0008 (0:00:54).

**Field key (one line per scene, engine 11.2):** SC ID | timecode | chapter | beat and INT tag | slot (E = evidence, I = illustrative) | claim IDs | NARR (final narration; numbers spelled out for the read) | W words written / target | ANCHOR (word -> visual) | SHOTS (visual events with rung/layer and tokens) | EV (visual events, R1 / R3) | RUNG (primary -> fallback) | FX | SRC (site, item URL where the stock research has one, and 2 exact English queries) | MOTION | TRANS | OST (on-screen text; "Illustrative" and source chips sit in the lower bar) | SFX | MUS | LOOK. The engine's R9 field (AI prompt, tokens, label) is left out of every line because the AI cap is 0 s.

**Standing rules for every line (S3 H, S4 8.2–8.7):**
- **Faces:** no face in any frame and no face in any mirror. Ramesh Babu appears only through CHR-01 devices; in this chapter (ages 7 to about 13) that means **objects only, never a child and never a human body part standing in for him** (S4 8.2). His father (CHR-02) is objects only; his mother (CHR-03) is hands and objects only; the relative who ran the shop (CHR-04) is typography only; his family (CHR-07) is "his family", never faces, names or a count. Stock people appear only as distant, anonymous city life and never over negative narration (death, hunger, poverty).
- **Era:** C1 objects stay timeless (worn leather, steel, paper, enamel, a bulb). No metro, glass towers, LED tools, smartphones, printed capes or brand labels under any 1970s–80s line. Period years appear only in R1 typography.
- **Money:** every rupee figure leads in dollars as a `$[C##]` placeholder until LEAD-03 fills it (C10 method: that year's FRED AEXINUS average, then CPI-U to the lock month), rupees shown small beneath it. No money figure appears in the hook (BTB-2: the method chip at SC0006 comes first). The export gate searches every text layer for "[" before render.
- **Quotes:** every statement of his is paraphrased with its outlet named, with a "Paraphrased" chip; no quotation cards.
- **Wording limits:** no birth year, no year or cause for his father's death, no uncle name and no villain, no name for his mother, no sibling count, no children, no "still owns".
- **Shop place:** no stock clip is ever labeled Brigade Road; the road exists only on the OSM map, which highlights the road, never a building. Shop interiors carry "Illustrative".

**Interrupt map (20 INTs; gaps 17–32 s; the hook's 11 cuts are hook density and not counted):** INT-01 V 0:00:38 · INT-02 Q 0:01:01 · INT-03 V 0:01:20 · INT-04 P 0:01:47 · INT-05 H 0:02:05 · INT-06 R 0:02:27 · INT-07 S 0:02:55 · INT-08 V 0:03:18 · INT-09 V 0:03:38 · INT-10 Q 0:03:55 · INT-11 H 0:04:25 · INT-12 P 0:04:55 · INT-13 R 0:05:17 · INT-14 V 0:05:45 · INT-15 S 0:06:04 · INT-16 P 0:06:28 · INT-17 R 0:06:50 · INT-18 H 0:07:13 · INT-19 S 0:07:40 · INT-20 V 0:08:02. Mix: 6 visual-mode (V), 3 silences (S), 3 sound hits (H), 3 perspective shifts (P), 2 on-screen questions (Q), 3 ramps or match cuts (R).

**Standing rules for every line in this chapter (S3 H, S4 8.2–8.7):**

**Word budget:** 970 narrated words written against a target of 975 (−0.5%). The target is 390 narrated seconds × 150 wpm, the S4 SND-VO average of 2.5 words per second. The chapter runs 420 s, and 30 s of it is wordless: SC0096, SC0110 and SC0136, 10 s each. A 10 s scene targets 25 words and an 11 s scene 27.5. Every scene sits within ±10% of its own target (the range is −5.5% to +4.0%), so no line asks the narrator to read faster than 156 wpm. SC0124 is written to 26 words because it carries a 0.6 s SIL-3 breath before "Intel."

**Chapter at a glance:**
- **Scenes:** 41 (SC0096–SC0136), contiguous from 0:15:30 to 0:22:30, with 0 s clock drift. RS06 has 21 scenes of 10 s. RS07 has 20 scenes: SC0117–SC0124, SC0128 and SC0134 run 11 s, the rest 10 s.

**Standing rules for every line in this chapter (S3 H, S4 8.2–8.7):**

**SC0118** | 0:19:11-0:19:22 | C3 | privacy, then the pivot · INT-09 V (0:19:15.4) | I | C28 (analysis) | NARR: She's a private person, so this film leaves her name out. But her idea turns the story. # BATCH: SC0229-SC0270 | 0:38:00-0:45:00 | Chapter C6 · Still at the Chair (S5, compact scene lines)

**Word budget:** 967 narrated words written against a target of 975 (−0.8%). The target is 390 narrated seconds × 150 wpm, the S4 SND-VO average of 2.5 words per second. Wordless time is 30 s: SC0229 (10 s), SC0258 (10 s) and SC0270 (10 s). The S3 brief assumed a 9 s end card and 978 words. The end card holds 10 s because S4's hold rule for its legal text, (words ÷ 3) + 1, comes to 10 s, so the target drops by 2.5 words. The payoff, SC0259–SC0265, is written about 5% lean (178 of 187.5 words) so it can be read at S4's 135–140 wpm with the SIL-3 breath. Every scene stays within ±10% of its own target (scene seconds × 2.5). By segment: RS14 390/387.5 · RS15 310/312.5 · PAYOFF 178/187.5 · CLOSE 89/87.5.

**Chapter at a glance:**
- **Scenes:** 42 (SC0229–SC0270), contiguous from 0:38:00 to 0:45:00, with 0 s clock drift.
- **Segments:** RS14 0:38:00–0:40:45 · RS15 0:40:45–0:43:00 · PAYOFF 0:43:00–0:44:15 · CLOSE 0:44:15–0:45:00.
- **Look:** LOOK-M + LOOK-C on all footage; LOOK-G on every graphic.
- **AI:** 0 s. Every visual event is R1 (built by the editor) or R3 (Pexels, Pixabay, Videvo Free license, Coverr downloaded by hand, Mixkit Free License).
- **Interrupts:** 17 (6 visual-mode, 2 silences, 2 hits, 3 perspective, 2 questions, 2 ramps or match cuts).
- **Visual events:** 75 (R1 28 / R3 47).
