**Field key (one line per scene, engine 11.2):** SC ID | timecode | chapter | beat and INT tag | slot (E = evidence, I = illustrative) | claim IDs | NARR (final narration, spoken numbers spelled out) | W words written / target | ANCHOR (word -> visual) | SHOTS (visual events, tokens) | EV (visual events in this scene, R1 / R3) | RUNG (rung/layer -> fallback) | FX | SRC (site, item URL where the stock research has one, and 2 exact English queries) | MOTION | TRANS | OST (on-screen text; chips and "Illustrative" sit in the lower bar) | SFX | MUS | LOOK. The engine's R9 field (AI prompt, tokens, label) is left out of every line because the AI cap is 0 s.

**Standing rules for every line in this chapter (S3 H, S4 8.2–8.7):**
- **Faces:** no face in any frame, and no mirror reflection of one. Ramesh Babu appears only through CHR-01 devices (G-H hands, G-S far silhouette, G-P POV, G-O objects, G-T typography), and every CHR-01 hands shot passes the S4 consistency line. Stock people appear only as distant, anonymous city life.
- **Cars:** every car under narration about his fleet carries "Illustrative" for the whole shot. Plates and legible signage are blurred in the N4 node. One car per sequence. The Ghost's color is never stated, and no Rolls-Royce clip appears in this chapter (10-minute window).
- **Money:** every dollar figure is a `$[C##]` placeholder until LEAD-03 fills it (C10 method: that year's FRED AEXINUS average, then CPI-U to the lock month). The rupee original is shown small beneath it. No dollar or rupee amount is spoken in this chapter: the narration names the price and the screen carries the dollar headline. The export gate searches every text layer for "[" before render.
- **Quotes:** every statement of his is paraphrased with its outlet named. There are no quotation cards, only a "Paraphrased" chip.
- **Wording limits:** no "still owns", no net-worth figure, no celebrity names, no children, no wife, and no plate number.

# Ramesh Babu: From Barber to Rolls-Royce (USA)

## S6: Whole-film QA report (engine section 13)

**Scope and method.** This report covers the final S5 film (`s5-C1.md` to `s5-C6.md`) after the fixes in `qa.json` / `fix-log.json`. Counts come from a script that parses every scene line (timecodes, NARR word counts, EV fields, INT tags, clip IDs). C1 and C5 were also read line by line against the S2 ledger. **C2, C3, C4 and C6 were checked by script and by their own S5 QA lines, not re-read line by line in this pass.** Nothing below claims a check that was not run.

# Ramesh Babu: From Barber to Rolls-Royce (USA)

## S7: Publishing pack (engine section 14)

Everything here matches the delivered film (`s5-C1.md` … `s5-C6.md`, 45:00, 270 scenes). Client-facing text is American English, with dollars first.

### 1. Title

**Final title (A): Why a Rolls-Royce Owner Still Cuts Hair**
- It stays in the present tense because the film supports it: SC0008 (0:00:54) and SC0238 (0:39:30), "as of the latest reports, in 2025" (C03). If a newer source says he has stopped, switch to "Why a Rolls-Royce Owner Kept Cutting Hair".
- **Test & Compare:** B "How Did a Barber Pay for a Rolls-Royce?" (paid at SC0204–SC0224) · C "One Rolls-Royce Day Equals How Many Haircuts?" (paid at SC0225, 0:37:20).

### 2. Thumbnails (1280×720; no faces of real people, no logos, no Rolls-Royce badge, grille or mascot; every image also appears in the film)

| ID | Pairs with | Image | Words | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TH-A | Title A | Open steel scissors on a worn counter in hard tungsten side light; out of focus behind, the long dark side of a sedan with a chrome handle (no badge). Thin red arrow to the scissors. Amber against teal-black. | HE KEPT THIS | Pexels 7697526 (razor hand, cropped, no face) or the SC0227 scissors clip; Pexels 9997960 sedan side, regraded dark, badge out of frame. Fallback: R1 line drawings. |
| TH-B | Title B | The film's ledger page from above, entries in ink, pen across it, one blank line circled in thick red (the SC0204 frame). | PLUS TAX | R1 ledger build (generic paper texture). |
| TH-C | Title C | Split: an empty vintage barber chair under one bulb / black car paint and a chrome door handle. A large white "=" and "?". Warm leather against cold chrome. | 1 DAY = | Chair: the SC0221 chair clip (not OBJ-03); handle: the SC0225 right-half clip. |

### 3. Description

> In 2011, by most accounts, a barber in Bengaluru, India, bought a Rolls-Royce Ghost for his rental fleet. As of the latest reports, in 2025, he still cuts hair. This is the story of Ramesh Babu, told through the money: from a five-rupee share of his late father's shop to a used van, a company, a first luxury car, and a road-tax bill that he said nearly bankrupted him.
>
> Every rupee figure is shown in US dollars at that year's exchange rate (FRED) and adjusted to today's money (BLS CPI-U). Where reports disagree, the film shows the range and names the outlets. His words are paraphrased, with the outlet named.
>
> **Chapters**
> 00:00 The Shop
> 08:30 The Chair
> 15:30 One Used Van
> 22:30 The Luxury Bet
> 30:00 The Ghost and the Bill
> 38:00 Still at the Chair
>
> **Sources** (as named on screen): YourStory (2014), The Better India, India TV, Cartoq, Deccan Chronicle (2016), DNA, News24, Zee News, Onmanorama (2018), Khaleej Times (2018), The Weekend Leader, the company's own website (rtt.co.in), and the public company registry (incorporation, 12 April 2004). Exchange rates: FRED series AEXINUS. Inflation: BLS CPI-U.
>
> Rolls-Royce, Mercedes-Benz and Maruti vehicles appear as illustrative details only, in editorial context; the film has no affiliation with or endorsement from any brand. No footage of Ramesh Babu or his family is used; people on screen are anonymous.

### 9. Posting plan (US audience)

- **Day 0:** publish the long film on a Thursday at 12:00 PM ET (9:00 AM PT), with Test & Compare running A, B and C (TH-A, TH-B, TH-C).
- **Day 0, +2 h:** pin the comment; post SH-01 as a Short linked to the film.
- **Day 2:** SH-02 (the haircut ratio) at 6:00 PM ET.
- **Day 4:** FB-01 on Facebook, 7:00 PM ET, captions burned in.
- **Day 6:** SH-03 (five rupees) at 12:00 PM ET.
- **Day 7:** read Test & Compare; keep the winner. If average view duration at 0:30 falls below 70%, re-cut the first 30 s and update the cover frame; do not change the title's promise.
- Reply to sourcing questions with links; never state a fleet size, net worth or celebrity client the film does not support.
